# Module index

Generated by `node scripts/gen-docs.mjs` from `docs/modules.json`, `docs/globals.json`,
`tests/suites/*.json`, the import graph and the sources themselves. Do not edit it:
`tests/docs-contract.mjs` regenerates it and fails on any difference. The frozen
system map is `docs/map-2026-10-05.md`; it is a snapshot, and `node scripts/map/audit.mjs`
reports how many of its `path:line` references still resolve.

805 files in 230 rows. A file belongs to the most specific row that names it (an exact path, else the glob with the most literal characters).

The suites column lists the suites whose import closure reaches the file or whose `covers`
in `tests/suites/<name>.json` name it, from those files' own fields; `**` covers are left out.

## Domains

- [site](#site): The landing page, articles and site-wide assets (36 files)
- [lab](#lab): The Econometrics Lab: courses, review, placement, projects and their storage (133 files)
- [worker](#worker): The Worker, its configuration, schema and migrations (51 files)
- [flows-server](#flows-server): Flows logic shared by the Worker, the pipeline and the browser bundles (117 files)
- [flows-rail](#flows-rail): The real-time rail (5 files)
- [flows-pipeline](#flows-pipeline): The nightly pipeline, the live leg and their tools (58 files)
- [flows-client](#flows-client): Flows pages, scripts and styles (66 files)
- [tooling](#tooling): Generators and authoring tools (31 files)
- [tests](#tests): Suites, helpers and fixtures (285 files)
- [ci](#ci): GitHub Actions workflows (12 files)
- [config](#config): Repository configuration (3 files)
- [docs](#docs): Documents and the generated index (8 files)

### `shared/markets.js`, `assets/js/market-ticker.js`

The landing index ticker: the Worker's Yahoo parse (the change is against the previous session's close taken from the same response's dated bars, never `chartPreviousClose`; each quote keeps its own `asOf`, `asOfDay`, `prevClose`, `prevDay` and, when the response names the session of the quote's own day, `sessionEnd`) and the strip that prints each quote's own İstanbul time, `Close <weekday>` for a closed market (a close is a quote struck within five minutes of the exchange's `sessionEnd`; the lag of the quote behind the fetch decides only where there is none), and a dash for a quote with no base.

| File | Exports | Suites |
|---|---|---|
| `assets/js/market-ticker.js` |  | market-ticker |
| `shared/markets.js` | `shared/markets.js#MARKET_CRON_STALE_MS`, `shared/markets.js#MARKET_INDICES`, `shared/markets.js#MARKET_STALE_MS`, `shared/markets.js#buildSnapshot`, `shared/markets.js#marketRefreshDue`, `shared/markets.js#parseIndexQuote` | flows, flows-basis, flows-dossier-reads, flows-engine-budget, flows-freshness, flows-ledger and 9 more |

### `_headers`

### `schema.sql`

D1 `users`, `progress`, `stats`, `mastery`, idempotent `mastery_attempts`, minimal `placement`, and per-owner `learning_sync` generation tables; the Flows tables, including `flows_live` (only `live:*` ids), `flows_tape`, `flows_clock`, the trigger that makes dated archive rows immutable and the two triggers that make `ideas:<date>` and `ideas-out:<date>` rows (and their `:r<n>` revisions) neither updatable nor deletable, with `shared/flows-archive.js` holding the write-once decision, its refusals (`archive_permanent` for these keys) and `PERMANENT_ARCHIVE_KEY_RE`; the model spend (`flows_ai_usage`, `flows_ai_usage_model`), board summary and Neuron (`flows_ai_summary`, `flows_neuron`) tables. `tests/academy-contract.mjs` builds three `node:sqlite` databases (this file, every `migrations/*.sql` in number order, the Worker's first-use DDL) and holds their tables, columns by name, CHECK clauses (literals compared verbatim), indexes, triggers and views equal; only `users`, `progress` and `stats` have no first-use DDL. Every `CREATE` and `DROP` of a table, index, trigger or view in `worker.js` and `shared/`, whatever its case or spacing, must sit inside a top-level DDL constant the suite evaluates, so inline DDL cannot escape the comparison. Every `ALTER TABLE` in `worker.js` and `shared/` must be one of the sites the suite lists, and each column it adds must be declared with the same type in this file and the migrations.

### `server/http.js`, `server/errors.js`, `server/log.js`, `shared/flows-log.js`, `tests/server-kernel.mjs`, `tests/server-log.mjs`

The Worker-only HTTP kernel. `http.js`: `HttpError` (with `HttpError.of(code, message?, headers?, details?)`), `json`, `apiError`, `redirect`, `requireMethod`, `requireSameOrigin`, `requireMutationOwner`, `requireReadOwnerIfPresent`, `readBounded`, `readJSON`, `tickerParam`, `requireTicker`, `keepAlive`, `errorText`, `internalKey(namespace, path)` (the `https://flows-<namespace>.internal/<path>` Cache API keys) and `edgeCache` (`handle`, `get`, `put`, each failure a miss). `errors.js`: `ERRORS`, one frozen `[status, default message]` per code. `log.js`: `logFailure(level, event, fields, error)`, the one way the Worker writes an unexpected failure. The shape is built by `shared/flows-log.js`, a leaf the shared modules can import: `failureEntry`, `failureLine` and `emitFailure(sink, level, event, fields, error)` write one JSON line `{ event, message, level, route, code, ...fields, error }`, with `message` an alias of `event` for one release, `level` `warn` or `error`, `route` the caller's route or path, `code` the caller's or the error's own string code, `error` the text of the caught value or null, and reserved keys that a field cannot rewrite. Logging never throws (an unserialisable field falls back to a minimal line, a sink that fails is ignored). The Worker's sites, the live layer's injected `log` sinks, the reading, the AI failover and the rail's one-a-minute log all go through it; success is never logged or sampled, so it adds no events. `tests/server-log.mjs` holds the shape, drives a real site of each module, and scans the closure for a hand-built failure line. `server/` holds no top-level mutable binding, no comments and is excluded from the static bundle. The suite runs every helper against a verbatim copy of the helper it replaced over tables of requests, bodies, origins and tickers, holds every literal `new HttpError(status, "code"` in `worker.js` and `shared/` to the table's status, and holds the failure-log key order. Literal `new HttpError(` sites stay where they are (the ingest scans pin some); a module moves to `HttpError.of` with its own move.

### `server/router.js`, `server/routes/flows-read.js`, `tests/server-router.mjs`

The Worker's route table, consulted first by `route()` and falling through to the remaining `if` chain. `createRouter(...families)` validates each row `{ id, path, methods, auth, handler }` (an absolute path and an id declared once, a non-empty list of HTTP methods, `auth` of `none` or `flows`, a handler), freezes it, and `handle(rc, gates)` returns `null` for a path with no row, else runs the method gate, then the session gate (`gates.flows`, a missing session being the 401 `unauthorized`), then the handler with `{ request, env, url, ctx, session }`: the order the Flows prefix block always had. `flowsReadRows(deps)` is the first family, 23 `GET` rows behind the Flows session: `board`, `brief`, `card`, `card-x`, `hist`, the overlaid `flowalerts`, `pulse` and `news`, and the fifteen plain nightly reads built by one `nightly(key, pending)` factory (`market`, `events`, `scoretrack`, `meta`, `political`, `unusual`, `movers`, `sectors`, `sector-premium`, `universe`, `regime`, `ideas`, `focus`, `roster`, `record`). The worker-local readers it needs (`readServed`, `readFlowsPayload`, `readWithOverlay`, `passthrough`, `recallLastGood`, `storeGone`, `absentKey`, `cardWithEngine`, `briefWithLive`, `nightlyFreshHeaders`) are injected, so `server/` imports nothing from `worker.js` or `cloudflare:`. A new route in this family is a row, never a new `if`; the ceiling enumeration in `flows-reads-contract` takes the table's paths plus the literals still in the chain (at least thirty together) and fails for any without a rows-read ceiling. The suite runs every row against a verbatim copy of the branch it replaced over eight stored-row worlds and seven store behaviours, comparing status, headers, body, errors and the order of calls.

### `server/routes/flows-ingest.js`, `server/store.js`, `server/schema.js`, `tests/server-ingest.mjs`

The flows-ingest family, the store and the schema registry. `flowsIngestRows(deps)` is one row, `/api/flows/ingest` by `GET`, `POST` and `DELETE`, whose credential is its own bearer token (the nightly, the static live token on a loopback host, or the live OIDC token) and not the Flows session; the method gate, the credential checks and the key and scope rules run in the order the branch had them. `server/store.js` is the only module that holds `flows_payload` SQL: `createFlowsStore({ ensureFlowsTables })` has `read`, `metadata`, `listing`, `remove`, `createArchive` and `write` (the nightly's `meta` lands its ledger row in the same batch; its upsert sets `chash`, `session`, `quality`, `epoch` and `asof` back to NULL and zero and bumps `rev`, so a row the v1 writer replaced never carries metadata that describes other bytes), and the door's `doorRead`, `doorWrite`, `doorBatch`, `have`, `planWrite`, `planRead` and `quarantine`, and exports `storedFrom`, `ingestKeyParts`, `INGEST_VIEW_KEY_RE` and the listing limits; `worker.js` reads the nightly rows through the same store. `server/schema.js` is the registry of the Flows statements, `{ name, table, ddl, addedColumns, migration, owner }`: the twelve the Worker has always sent (built from the DDL each domain exports plus the six tables that were literals in `worker.js`, in the order it sent them), `flows_ai_outcome` and `flows_ai_reject` after them, then `flows_quarantine` and the three `flows_payload_manifest_*` triggers; `FLOWS_SCHEMA_SQL` is derived from it. `upgradeColumns(db, table, added, known?)` replaces `upgradeClockColumns` for every table: it adds only the columns a table lacks, tolerates `duplicate column`, rethrows anything else, and takes names and types only in the shapes `checkAdded` allows. `applySchema(db, registry)` is the bootstrap `ensureFlowsTables` runs: one batch of the CREATEs and one `PRAGMA table_info` per table that has added columns (today `flows_payload` and `flows_clock`, so twenty-two statements), then the upgrades. A new column on a registered table is one entry in its `addedColumns` list (`PAYLOAD_ADDED_COLUMNS`, `CLOCK_ADDED_COLUMNS`, `LEDGER_ADDED_COLUMNS`, which is empty today) plus the same column in the table's DDL, `schema.sql` and a migration, and it is added at first use instead of being refused by `batchWithLedger`. The suite runs the row against a verbatim copy of the branch it replaced over 7 credentials, 6 methods, 39 queries and 5 bodies (statements, call order, errors, bodies and the rows left behind), checks the store, the registry against the migrations and `schema.sql` (twenty statements), `upgradeColumns` against hostile names, races and refused batches, and the Worker scans. The door itself is `server/door.js` and `shared/flows-door.js`, below.

### `shared/flows-door.js`, `server/door.js`, `tests/flows-door-v2.mjs`

The ingest door v2 in warn mode (B3a and B3), behind `FLOWS_DOOR_V2` (`wrangler.toml` [vars], shipped `off`; only the exact word `warn` turns it on, so `enforce` is `off` until B3b). With the switch off the ingest route is byte for byte what it was, and `?op=` and `X-Content-Hash` are ignored. With `warn`, a non-archive, non-live write is inspected, its row gets metadata, and nothing is refused. `shared/flows-door.js` is the pure leaf (it imports `flows-live.js` and `flows-freshness.js` only): `canonicalize` (keys sorted; the volatile stamps `generatedAt`, `readAt`, `screenerReadAt`, `refreshedAt`, `producer` and `fresh.readAt` dropped; non-finite numbers and nesting past 64 levels named), `sha256Hex`, `inspect(key, text, { now })` (per-product structural findings derived from the dry-run emit, every payload of which passes with none: `not_object`, `missing_key`, `schema_version`, `depth`, `panel_shape`, `ticker_grammar`, `ticker_mismatch`, `session_format`, `instant_format`, `session_future` against the next session the calendar expects, `n_mismatch`, `column_length`, `units_missing`, `rows_type`, `row_shape`; range violations in the universe are flags that cost quality and are never structural; a pending placeholder has no required keys), the ordering tuple `(epoch, session, quality, asof)` with `quality = 4 x round(63 x ok base panels / base panels) + tier`, so a complete L1 (253) beats an L2 that misses a base panel (235) and a complete L2 (255) beats a complete L1, `casVerdict` (`new`; `unchanged` by canonical hash; `advance` on an equal or higher tuple; `superseded`), `regressionOf` (a later session's card that keeps under three quarters of the stored base panels), `assess` (adds `size_collapse` under 40 per cent of the stored size for the products whose size follows the universe, `stale_producer` and `hash_mismatch`), `membershipFlag`, `planOf` and `doorMode`. `server/door.js` is `createDoor({ store, ensureFlowsTables, storeGone, log })`: `write` (one batch reading the key's stored tuple and, for a ticker key, the universe's name list, then one write; the response gains `door { mode, chash, product, session, quality, epoch, asof, cas, would, flags, ring }`), and `operation` for `?op=batch` (up to 100 keys or 2 MiB, each judged on its own; archive keys, `live:` keys, `meta` and duplicates refused per item; writes in D1 batches of 16 that are bisected to the poison key when the store refuses one deterministically, and not when the failure looks transient, at most forty failed batches), `?op=have` (up to 90 `(key, chash)` pairs in one statement; answers `have` and `need`) and `?op=plan` (the nightly's planned card set for a session, kept as the row `plan`; an older session never replaces a newer one). Every would-have-refused finding lands in `flows_quarantine`, a ring of 200 rows (id is the position, pruned by id range; bodies of at most 64 KiB kept for the newest twenty; a per-producer brake that keeps no body after twenty events of one check in the newest forty). The regression guard only logs its count (`door would hold regressions`); a ring that cannot be written is logged and never fails an ingest. `X-Producer-Id` names the producer and `X-Producer-Epoch` declares its epoch. The enforcing compare-and-set SQL exists in the store (`doorWrite` with `enforce`) and is proved equal to `casVerdict` over every pair of 36 tuples, but no route passes it. The synthetic and fixture refusal is another row's hook and is not here. `tests/flows-door-v2.mjs` reads the dry-run emit and runs the leaf, the store and the route over the warn paths, the batch, `have` and `plan`, the off paths, the upgrade of an old database, the manifest triggers and the round trip off, warn, off, warn with row-content assertions, and plants twenty-one wrong builds that its batteries must fail.

### `server/routes/flows-pages.js`, `tests/server-pages.mjs`

The flows-pages family in the route table: `flowsPageRows(deps)` is the sign-in page, `/flows`, the fourteen gated pages (`/flows/`, `long`, `short`, `watch`, `market`, `history`, `desk`, `strategy`, `ask`, `ticker`, `unusual`, `events`, `track`, `political`) and a 308 row for the bare form of each page but Home, all `GET` and `HEAD` with `auth: none`, because an anonymous visitor is answered with the sign-in page and HTTP 200, never a 401. The page list is one array, and the bare redirects and the `?t=` reader redirect (a 302 to the ticker page from Home, `long`, `short` and `watch`, before any session is read) are generated from it. A page row reads the session through the injected `currentFlowsUser`, for Home alone the board summary through `readFlowsSummary`, and then asks the injected `isOwner(env, username)` whether the member is an owner, which `market` and `unusual` take as `owner`. The public front door is two more rows with a 308 row for each bare form: `/flows/about/` and `/flows/glossary/`, answered to a signed-out reader with no session read, and the Flows 404 when `FLOWS_FRONT_DOOR` is `off` (a method other than `GET` and `HEAD` is the row's 405 first, where the chain answered 404 while the front door was off). The page functions are `shared/flows-pages.js`'s, and the family reads no store and names no vendor or model. `POST /flows/login` and `/flows/logout` stay in the chain for the auth family. The suite runs every row against a verbatim copy of the branch it replaced over every path, query, method, member and front-door setting (84,000 checks) and holds the Worker's table, scans and the absence of the old branches.

## flows-server

Flows logic shared by the Worker, the pipeline and the browser bundles.

### `shared/flows-ai-broker.js`, `server/ai.js`, `tests/flows-ai-broker.mjs`

The AI broker and its outcome telemetry. `shared/flows-ai-broker.js` is pure: the four surfaces (`board`, `neuron`, `ask`, `read`), the fourteen outcomes (`clean`, `trimmed`, `refused`, `unparsable`, `overlong`, `empty`, `length`, `budget`, `class`, `user`, `allowance`, `capacity`, `plan`, `unreachable`), `chainFor`, `transportOutcome` (a reply with no text), `guardOutcome` (the stored `llm` and `guard` of a surface mapped to an outcome and a low-cardinality reason), `tokenOf` (a culprit, 40 characters, no control or bidi characters) and `foldCounters` (the owner block's shape: per surface and model, calls, outcomes, the share of calls that reached a model whose wording failed, mean and max latency, and the fifty latest refusals). `server/ai.js` is the I/O: `aiCall(env, deps, { surface, chain, messages, opts, onUsage })` runs `askModels(deps.ai(), ...)`, times it, settles a reply with no text itself and returns the reply with `call.settle(verdict, culprit)` for the surface to call once it has judged the wording (a second settle does nothing). A settle upserts one `flows_ai_outcome` row (day, surface, model, outcome, reason: `n`, `ms_sum`, `ms_max`) and, for a wording failure with a culprit, writes `flows_ai_reject (surface, slot 0..49)` in the same batch; it never throws, and a store that refuses it is logged once (`ai outcome not recorded`). `readAiBlock(env, deps, { days })` is the owner block in one trip (today's counters; up to seven days behind `days`) and `pruneAiOutcomes` keeps eight days, run in the 03:00 ET window. The block is mounted by the health and owner-view row (P1-27). Nothing in it reads the binding or runs a model: `shared/flows-ai.js` stays the only place. The suite covers the outcome table, the fold, `aiCall` against a scripted binding and a counting D1 (one trip a call, two rows for a refusal with a culprit, a ring that wraps at fifty, a refused or missing store, the budget refusal counted without the model asked), the block's trips and rows, retention, the registry and migration 0017 parity, and the scans.

### `shared/flows-stats.js`, `tests/gen-stats-fixtures.py`, `tests/fixtures-stats.json`, `tests/flows-stats-contract.mjs`

The statistics leaf: no imports, no clock, no randomness beyond the seeded `mulberry32`. Mean, sample variance and deviation, median, quantiles, share at or below, average-rank percentile, Pearson and Spearman, the Wilson interval, effective n under clustering, the t critical value and the mean interval, the Newey-West standard error, a seeded stationary bootstrap and its interval, Brier, log score, the reliability table with the Murphy decomposition (reliability - resolution + uncertainty + within-bin variance - 2 x within-bin covariance, exact for binned forecasts), Benjamini-Hochberg. A null never becomes a zero: an empty or non-numeric input answers `null`. `gen-stats-fixtures.py` (scipy, numpy, statsmodels; the versions and the generator's SHA-256 are in the fixture) writes the committed JSON the contract reads without Python. Consumers: `shared/flows-vol.js`, `shared/flows-features.js`, `shared/flows-cross.js`, and the medians of `shared/flows-political.js`, `flows-premium.js`, `flows-dossier.js`, `flows-variation.js`, `flows-market.js` and `scripts/flows-quant-pipeline.mjs`.

### `shared/flows-conviction.js`, `tests/flows-conviction.mjs`, `tests/gen-conviction-fixtures.py`, `tests/fixtures-conviction.json`, `tests/conviction-sim.mjs`

Does conviction order outcomes (P2-22): a pure leaf over `flows-record.js`'s forward-close rule that scores each archived board name as a hit when it beat the session's equal-weight board average in its board's direction, fits `logit P(hit) = a + b·cnv/100` with a session-clustered sandwich and Student t on G-1 degrees of freedom, reads terciles of `cnv` with Wilson intervals on the effective count, the clustered top-minus-bottom gap and its minimum detectable size (2.8 standard errors), the pooled Spearman with a leave-one-session-out jackknife, and breadth's own coefficient. `evaluateConviction` returns `keep` (slope interval above zero and rising terciles), `relabel` (the number is an "Agreement index") or `pending` (under 60 scored sessions and 600 rows at the ten-session horizon, or no fit), published as `record.conviction`. The contract holds the estimators to numpy and scipy on 500 committed reference rows (`gen-conviction-fixtures.py` writes the JSON) and to simulated boards with a known slope. The History page that displayed it is removed.

### `shared/flows-time.js`, `tests/flows-time.mjs`

The Türkiye-time oracle, a leaf over `flows-freshness.js` alone. TRT is fixed UTC+3 arithmetic labelled with the literal `TRT` (Türkiye has no daylight time; the test proves it equal to `Europe/Istanbul` at every half hour of 2026-2028). `instantOf` takes a finite positive number, a `Date` or an ISO string with a time and an explicit `Z` or offset and answers null for a date-only, zone-less or impossible value, so a reader prints the dash; `clock`, `stamp`, `day` and `when` print TRT; `axisMinutes(v, sessionDay)` is minutes since 00:00 TRT of the session day, so a chart axis stays strictly increasing across TRT midnight when the winter close is 00:00, and `tick(m)` prints it modulo a day (never 24:00). `boundaries(day, clock)` derives pre-market, open, close and after-hours end in TRT only from `sessionOpen`, `sessionClose`, `easternInstant` and `closeMinutes`: 16:30 to 23:00 in US summer time, 17:30 to 00:00 in standard time, early closes 20:00 and 21:00; `sessionSaid`, `phaseSaid` and `nextOpenSaid` are the wordings. No importer yet, no asset. `TRT` is also in the Worker's `ASK_NOT_TICKERS`, because it is a real ticker.

### `shared/flows-plan-facts.js`, `tests/flows-plan-facts.mjs`

Every platform cap per plan in one table, a leaf that imports nothing. `RESOURCES` is the meter list (vendor day and minute, D1, Worker, Durable Object, Workers AI, Analytics Engine, R2, Queues, Logs, GitHub), `FACTS` the other caps; each cell carries its source id (`SOURCES`: page, how it was read, date) and the Free cell is the downgrade column, the Paid cell the design column. `FLOWS_PLAN_WORKERS` is `free` or `paid` (unset: `paid`; a misspelt value: `free`), `FLOWS_UW_PLAN` is `unknown`, `advanced`, `startup`, `startup-annual` or `kafka` (unset or unrecognised: `unknown`, the minimum of each vendor row). `lines(env)` is the one view a governor or a budget test reads; no other file reads either variable. `renewalState` reports the Workers Paid period and does not change a line.

### `shared/flows-live.js`, `shared/flows-live-worker.js`

The live layer's key registry and pure builders; the Worker's Tier 1 tick, dispatch, watchdog, live ingest, `/api/flows/lk`, `/now`, `/tape` and read-time overlays. A strip row ends in `qa`, the vendor's `quote_time` as seconds behind the read, and the key's `ahead { n, maxS }` counts the stamps that run later than the read; `priorCloseBase` and `shapeStrips` fill a null `prev_close` from the last dated nightly close and say so in `prevFill`, and hold out a row the vendor dates before the session as an all-null row (DEPLOY.md 10.5i). `live:alerts:head` is the compact head of the same alerts record (the 20 largest-premium windows as facts, at most 8 KiB, breadth class, Actions writer): `shapeAlertsHead` builds it from the merged `live:alerts` payload the pass has just written, stamps it with that record's own `readAt` and vendor cursor, makes no vendor call, and `live.mjs` writes it only when `live:alerts` itself was written; it is the input compact enough for a model job to read without parsing the 120 KiB record.

### `shared/flows-basis.js`, `tests/flows-basis-contract.mjs`

The premium desk's basis: the vendor's `{data:{...}}` stock-state envelope, a spot that is only a regular-session price (`printOf`: the live print in the regular session, else the newest regular close), and the coherence gate between that spot and the chain (`coherence`: impossible asks, the strike bracket the `maybe_otm_only` request guarantees, one underlying fitted to the nearest expiries, a rebase past 0.25% or a refusal when the fit is too thin). The contract runs the Worker route in Node against a stubbed vendor. The desk's `asOf` is the New York date of the READ and `days` count from it; `UW_NOW` (an ISO instant, honoured only while `UW_BASE` redirects the vendor away from production) pins that read clock so the workerd suites' dated fixtures stay valid.

### `shared/flows-vendor-ops.js`

The vendor operation registry, a data-only leaf: one entry for each of the 75 operations production reads, with `id`, `path` (the call template; `op` is the spec's operation when the two differ), `plan`, `licence`, `envelope`, `klass` (a `PACKET_CLASSES` name), `limiter` (`ondemand` for an operation the Worker serves on demand, `null` for one only an engine reads), `liveOnly`, `maxBytes` and `reads` (the fields the code reads, which the weekly probe holds strict); `opById`, `pathFor(id, params)` (each parameter is encoded as one path segment and a missing one is refused), `opsFor(limiter)`, `BUDGETS` (the consumer ceilings and windows) and `PLAN` (the observed key and the Startup plan). No vendor path is spelled anywhere else in `worker.js`, `shared/` or `scripts/` outside the fakes, `scripts/flows-probe.mjs` derives its strict read list from here, and `tests/flows-probe-contract.mjs` scans for a stray literal, holds every entry to the committed spec or `liveOnly`, and holds each `BUDGETS` figure equal to the code constant it names.

### `shared/flows-tape-stats.js`, `tests/flows-tape-stats.mjs`

The option-print classifier, a leaf that imports nothing and reads no clock: `tierOf` sorts a fill against the NBBO into A (at or above the ask), a, m (the midpoint), b, B (at or below the bid) or n (no usable quote); `sideTagOf` reads the vendor's one side tag and `classifyPrint` returns the row (`side` -1, 0 or +1, `tier`, `tag`, `parity`, `basis`, a `flags` bitmask, `signed`). Where the tag and the tier disagree the print is unsigned and counted, never guessed. `sweepOf` reads both encodings of a sweep, `report_flags` containing `intermarket_sweep` or an upstream code (`upstream_condition_detail` on REST, `trade_code` on the socket) containing the whole token `isoi`, and never the equity enum. Multi-leg codes, canceled prints and the `dividend` and `arbitrage` tags keep a print out of every signed total; a large print exists only as ten times a premium floor the caller states. `aggregate` sums asked, bid and unclassified premium over rolling windows and `reconciles` holds them to gross within 1e-9; `askSplit`, `askShare` and `askShareRead` are the one ask-share definition, the rule of `tests/fixtures-ask-share.json` (a side counts only as a non-negative number, a window needs a positive premium, sides past the premium are scaled to it, windows are pooled, the coverage is sided dollars over every positive premium, and 0.65, 0.35 and 0.5 are compared as the whole percents printed). `tests/flows-tape-stats.mjs` runs synthetic tapes only, because the repository is public, and reads `tests/fixtures-ask-share.json` when that table is in the tree.

### `shared/flows-premium.js`, `tests/flows-premium-contract.mjs`

The premium desk's ranking: gates, annualization, the strike divisor and `rankChain`. Its mid implied volatility inverts against a per-expiry forward (`forwardPlan`): the chain's own put-call parity over up to ten near-the-money two-sided pairs (mid of at least 0.05, spread under half the mid) when there are three or more, else the card's implied carry (`deskCarry`: grade 2 or better, and only when `coherence` returned `ok`), else the rate alone. A parity forward or a carry whose log gap from the rate forward exceeds `forwardGapLimit(years)` (2% plus 0.5 a year, capped at 15%) is not believed. A rebased chain takes no carry because its fitted spot has already absorbed the carry over the fit horizon, the nearest expiries, only: later expiries of a rebased chain keep the rate-only forward unless parity places them, so their mid IV stays biased (about 1.1 vol points at 180 days and 2.1 at 360 days at a 3% carry on an out-of-the-money-only chain), a limit pinned in `tests/flows-basis-contract.mjs`. `rankChain` returns the choice per expiry as `forwards` (an empty list on a `mismatch` payload).

### `shared/flows-quant-dealer.js`

The dealer gamma profile (`gammaProfile`): a leaf with no imports. Per contract it precomputes `1/(sigma sqrt(T))`, the affine part of d1 in ln x and the signed weight, so each grid point is one logarithm and one exponential per contract instead of a full set of Black-Scholes greeks; output equals the greeks-based profile to 1e-12 of the profile's peak and the flip to 1e-9, at about a tenth of the CPU (`tests/flows-quant.mjs` holds the equality against a reference copy and the CPU ratio through `tests/lib/cpu-budget.mjs`).

### `shared/flows-ai-salvage.js`, `tests/flows-ai-salvage.mjs`

The salvage leaf for a model reply that was cut off. `parseReadingPartial(text)` is a single-pass scanner (string, escape and depth state, no recursion, no eval) that returns every array element and top-level member that closed before the cut, marked `truncated`; `salvageReading(text, dossier, tags, opts)` vets that exactly as `vetReading` vets a whole reply, discards the model's own `unknown` list and puts `unknownEntries(dossier)` in its place with the note that the list of what is not known is the site's own, still needs `now` and a second section, and names `read:salvaged:<kept>` as its guard. `opts.trim` (off by default) also keeps the element cut inside its cites, cut back at a sentence boundary to the sentences the closed cites support and carrying only the cites a surviving sentence uses. It imports `flows-reading.js` and `flows-ask.js`, nothing imports it yet (the queue consumer and the output budget are F-45), and it makes no model call. The suite cuts nine valid replies at every UTF-8 byte offset and checks the items against the whole reply, the growth of the set, the sentence-boundary trims and the unknown list, and plants six wrong builds.

### `shared/flows-targets.js`, `tests/flows-targets.mjs`

The canonical large-move targets, a leaf with no imports: the frozen definition table (35 ids: `move.k{2,3,4}.h{1,5}` with `.up` and `.dn`, `move.im{1,1.5,2}.h1`, `move.x{5,10,20}.h{1,5}`, `move.earn.im`, `move.earn.ratio`, `move.ret.h{1,5,21}` at the fifteen-quantile union, `move.live.res` as a reading, `move.z63.h{1,5}` as a sensitivity), its `TARGETS_VERSION` and `targetsHash` (a definition changed without a version bump fails the test's pinned history), `sigmaRef` (1.4826 x MAD of the trailing 252 regular-session, split-adjusted, break-free, non-event log returns, at least 200 else the name is not forecast and counted with the reason, `clockVersion` `sess-v0` with the weekend variance 0; a later estimate is a new id for new issues only), `impactSession(report_date, report_time)` (defined once, here), `threshold` and `resolves` (every event row carries a scale and a finite k, `move.earn.im` on `sigmaQE` of the impact session; a missing scale is `{ok:false, reason:"no scale"}` and resolves to null; the vendor's `implied_move_perc` is refused as a source), `earnRatio` within one track, `rarityText` (a k-multiple is always printed with how often such days occur for that name, the empirical route floored at 1 in 250, the model route at the audited `pFloor` it is given, the scan p printed `p below 0.00002` and never as 1 in N) and `cardField` (about 30 bytes a card). 670 names take about 40 ms of thread CPU.

### `shared/flows-moves0.js`, `tests/flows-moves0.mjs`

The IV-days lens on Market's Extremes (Risers and Fallers, `Raw change` and `In IV-days`, on for the owner's account and off for members, the worker passing `rtIsOwner` to `marketPage` as `data-lens`) and the IV-days column in Home's Leaders. `m = chg / (iv30p / sqrt(252))`, with `iv30p` the previous trading day's 30-day IV (the vendor's `iv30d_1d`, never a current IV, which rises with the move and would rank the largest shocks lowest) and the vendor's `implied_move_perc` never a unit; a name with `iv30p` null or not positive is on no list, never zero; a name with earnings inside 21 sessions is ranked apart; the median and the count beyond 3 are over the clean names; one line of copy (`A ranking, not a test: no error rate measured.`) and the caption `IV as of the previous close.`, none of the words unusual, alert, screen or rare. The leaf is the oracle and the pages carry the one division, since a page cannot import a module; the test holds both pages to its digits. Market ranks one population at a time and names it: the whole nightly universe once the nightly for the live rows' session has landed, the live roster alone while the live session is newer, the nightly when the live rows are older or unavailable. Home reads `/api/flows/universe?cols=t,sec,chg,iv30p,ed` (14.5 KB of JSON for 670 names, about 3.3 KB gzipped) for the event mark and the nightly fallback. The order is held while a mouse pointer is on the module. Chromium against `page.route` stubs, no server.

### `shared/flows-cross.js`

The `universe` payload's column store (`UNIVERSE_COLUMNS`, one integer column per key, decoded by `units`) and its 100 KiB budget, shed by column priority when over. The nightly's dealer columns are dollars per 1% move over average daily dollar volume (`gexAdv`, `dex`, `vanna`, `charm`) and keep the vendor's sign; `im5` and `im30` are the vendor's implied-move fractions. On 670 synthetic names the payload is 96,158 bytes without them, 108,249 with them unbudgeted, and 100,505 after the shedder drops `adx`, `dGamma` and `gexRatio`; `iv30p` (the vendor's `iv30d_1d`, the previous trading day's 30-day implied volatility, the denominator of the IV-days ranking) adds 2,681 bytes, so the same 670 names are 111,139 unbudgeted and 100,609 after the shedder drops `adx`, `dGamma`, `gexRatio` and `dIv1m` (priority 5, no reader). `iv30p` is priority 1 and is never shed, and `charm` is priority 3 so the Neuron screen keeps it; `tests/flows-legs-contract.mjs` holds both on the 670-name fixture. The run's `shed` list is the measurement that counts. `projectionColumns` and `projectUniverse` serve `GET /api/flows/universe?cols=t,sec,chg,iv30p,ed` (one to twelve column names, normalised and sorted, a 400 `invalid_cols` otherwise): the asked columns of the one payload row, about 14.5 KB for 670 names and five columns, kept in the Cache API for 300 s so a repeat read costs 0 D1 rows, never written over the whole universe's last good copy.

### `shared/flows-activity.js`, `scripts/flows-legs/activity.mjs`

The Unusual page's dated exchange-code unit. After the chain pass the nightly makes one call, `/api/option-activity/unusual` with `date` the session, `limit` 200 (the vendor's page maximum) and the largest premium first; `CALL_COST.activity` counts it, and `FLOWS_UNUSUAL_V2=off` makes no call and publishes `activity.status: "off"`. `buildActivityRows` keeps a row whose option symbol parses, whose volume is positive and whose last fill falls on the session's Eastern day (another day is counted in `offDate`; no fill time is kept and counted in `undated`); a sweep, floor, multi-leg, stock multi-leg or cross volume (`ACTIVITY_CLASSES` order) that is negative or above the row's volume is withheld as null for that class only. The classes overlap and are never summed. `mergeActivity` puts `cls` and `pm` (executed premium) on the ranked contract rows that are also in the screen, joined by the pipeline's own contract id; an unmatched row carries neither key. The payload gains `activity` (status, `asOf`, counts, at most 30 rows and 3 a name) and `basis.activity`; `volumeAsOf` stays null because the chain counter is still undated. The page adds the row description and a list of the screen's largest by premium. The lexicon test names "sweep" as an exception for `basis.activity` alone.

### `shared/flows-dispersion.js`, `scripts/flows-legs/dispersion.mjs`, `tests/flows-dispersion.mjs`

Correlation and dispersion context for the Nasdaq-100. The leaf (imports only `flows-stats.js`) turns QQQ's holdings weights, the index's and members' 30-day implied volatilities and daily closes into the implied correlation (the weighted average pairwise correlation that reproduces the index's implied variance), the realised one over 21 and 63 sessions (the same quantity on the members' return series, equal to the brute-force weighted average of pairwise correlations), their difference and the index-to-members volatility ratio, each with its coverage: a member reporting inside the 30 days is excluded from the implied side and counted, a member with a gap or no movement in the window from the realised side. The leg reads members' bars from the run's candles and fetches closes for the rest at most 100 a week, caching them in the row's `state` (the Worker drops it); a new ISO week starts a new budget.

### `shared/flows-ready.js`, `tests/flows-ready.mjs`

The readiness leaf, a module over `flows-time.js` and `flows-freshness.js` alone. `readiness(state, now)` answers for the current session whether Flows can serve the open: the nightly landed for `expectedNightlySession` (the run record `meta`, then `universe`, `regime`, `events` and both `board` sides each at or beyond it), Tier 1 ticking in the session (`tier1At` within 11 minutes, `tier1OkAt` within 16, a holiday verdict skipped, an 11-minute grace after the open), the rail when the caller is an owner and the day is a trading day inside 04:00-20:00 ET (off, unreachable, degraded or a roster error each block), and the clock row. It returns `ok`, `blockers` (`code` and a plain `sentence`), `checks` (`ok`, `blocked` or `skipped` each), `expected`, the session the open belongs to, `minutesToOpen` and `opens` in TRT through `flows-time.js`. `healthOf` is the three-field public body. A null or unreadable input is a blocker, never a pass. `tests/flows-ready.mjs` holds each blocker from both sides at a pinned clock, the pre-market news cadence and its kill switch, and the one-call-one-row write.

### `server/routes/flows-ready.js`

The ready family in the route table: `flowsReadyRows(deps)` is `GET /api/flows/ready` behind the Flows session and the anonymous `GET /api/health`. Readiness reads the clock row and one `store.metadata` call for the six nightly keys (about six rows and two trips, never a payload), asks the rail (`railReadiness` in `shared/flows-rt-routes.js`, a 1.5 s read of the object's own status) only for an owner, and answers `shared/flows-ready.js`'s body with `owner`. `FLOWS_READY` and `FLOWS_HEALTH` in `wrangler.toml` are `on`; anything else is a 404 `ready_off` or `health_off`. Health is `{ok, asOf, session}` computed from the calendar, no store, no clock row, no rail. The pre-market news pass (`premarketNewsTick` in `shared/flows-live-worker.js`, `FLOWS_PREMARKET_NEWS`) runs on the half-hourly housekeeping firing between 04:00 and 09:30 ET on trading days, makes one vendor call (the news headlines, limit 100) and upserts `live:news` for the day's session, at most 11 calls and 11 rows a day (`BUDGETS.premarketNews`).

### `server/routes/flows-dispatch.js`, `tests/flows-dispatch-check.mjs`

The owner-only dispatch credential check: `flowsDispatchRows(deps)` is `POST /api/flows/dispatch-check` behind the Flows session. A non-owner is `403 owner_only` (`FLOWS_RT_USERS` through `isOwner`), a cross-origin call `403`, and a second call inside 60 s in one isolate `429 rate_limited` with `Retry-After: 60` (`state.dispatchCheckAt`). `checkDispatchCredential` in `shared/flows-live-worker.js` makes one read-only `GET /repos/{repo}/actions/workflows/{FLOWS_LIVE_WORKFLOW}` with the Worker's token (200 `ok`, 401 `bad-token`, 403 and 404 `no-access`, a network failure `unreachable`) and one `dispatchWorkflow` of the live workflow with `{origin: manual, drill: true}`, which opens one witness issue and closes it at once under the concurrency group `flows-live-drill`. The outcome is written to `flows_clock.dispatch_why` through `recordDispatchWhy` (one upsert, failure ignored, nothing written for a missing token). The body is `{ok, read, dispatch, recorded, repo, workflow, at}` and never carries the token or a header value. `tests/flows-dispatch-check.mjs` drives `worker.js` with a scripted GitHub for seven status pairs, the token-absent and store-failure cases, every principal and the rate limit, and fails seven planted wrong builds of the route.

### `shared/flows-fixture-names.js`, `scripts/flows-legs/taint.mjs`, `tests/fixture-guard.mjs`, `tests/flows-fixture-guard-render.mjs`

The fixture guard (SYN-01 to SYN-11 of the 2026-10-13 synthetic audit): nothing invented can reach a member. `shared/flows-fixture-names.js` is the reserved namespace, a leaf that imports nothing: `FIXTURE_TICKER_RE` (SY, one letter, three digits, or GONE and one digit, which no exchange symbol can match because symbols hold no digits), `FIXTURE_TOKEN_RE` (a whole JSON string, never a word inside a headline), `isFixtureTicker`, `fixtureNameIn`, `fixtureMarked` (a top-level `fixture: true`), `fixtureHost` (loopback or a `.test` name, the only waiver), `fixtureRefusal(key, text)` and `FIXTURE_REFUSALS` (the 422 `fixture_ticker` and `fixture_payload` rows `server/errors.js` repeats). Worker door: `server/routes/flows-ingest.js` `contentRefusal(url, key, text)` refuses a fixture ticker key on every method, a body naming one and a stamped body before any D1 statement, and passes the request host into `ingestLive` for live keys; it is the one seam a later door (schema, compare-and-set, quarantine) extends. The read side answers 400 `invalid_ticker` for a fixture name off a test host (`requireTicker`, the summary route, the rail's `f`). No variable can switch the guard off: only the request host waives it. Pipeline: `publish()` in `scripts/flows-nightly/store.mjs` refuses before the network (`publishRefusal`: a fixture name, a stamped body, or `fixture_code` when `scripts/flows-legs/taint.mjs` says a fake ran) unless the ingest host is a test host, sets the exit code and throws an error with `fixtureLeak`, which `createStageRunner.run` rethrows even for an isolated stage; every dry-run payload that is an object is written with `fixture: true`; `targetRefusal` stops a run whose `FLOWS_UW_BASE_URL` is a redirect or invalid base from publishing to a non-test ingest, before the first vendor call, nightly and live. `taint.mjs` is a leaf (`touch`, `tainted`, `clearTaint`, the last for tests only); every export of `fixtures.mjs` and each fake constructor touches it on entry. Backstops: the health gate adds a `fixture-name` failure when any live body it already reads names a fixture; the client (`FlowsUI.fixture` in `assets/js/flows-ui.js`, a copy of the token and host rules the suite holds equal to the leaf) quarantines a page that is sent a fixture name or `fixture: true` (hidden main, the unavailable pill, one console error, later Flows requests held), drops rail frames that carry one and counts them in `stats.fixture`, and refuses a fixture `?t=` before asking for a card; `scripts/render-preview.mjs` runs on a `.test` origin and prints a Fixture data ribbon on every page. `tests/fixture-guard.mjs` (class N) holds the namespace table with five wrong rules, the Worker door and the pipeline battery against copies of the source with planted wrong builds each of which must fail, the dry-run stamp, the stage rethrow, the configuration preflight as spawned processes, the health line, the static confinement scan (a fixture token only in tests, docs, the fakes, the preview fixtures and the leaf; a fake called outside a dry-run branch; every fake taints on entry; only tests clear the taint) with its own mutations, the Vision sample-world strings absent from served files, and the dry emit stamped. `tests/flows-fixture-guard-render.mjs` (class C, `page.route`, no server) renders the long board and ticker from the dry emit on the production origin and on `.test` and loopback origins, the rail on both, a stamped real-named card, and five planted wrong builds of the served scripts.

## flows-rail

The real-time rail.

### `shared/flows-rt.js`

The real-time rail's pure half: the frozen envelope, topic table, row columns and close codes, the owner switches (`rtSwitches`), `streamEntry` (the `entryOf` shape plus the lag guard), the parse of the 256-byte client messages, per-topic sequence counter and `createSeq` gap detector, the 240-call budget, lag statistics, the latest-wins and append-dedupe merges, the REST shapers `shapePx`, `shapeFl`, `shapeGx`, `shapeMk`, `shapeNw` (vendor body to neutral frame, calling the stored live keys' own shapers), `mergeTopic`, and `RT_UPSTREAM`, the one table from topic to REST request and to vendor socket channels.

### `shared/flows-rt-socket.js`

The vendor socket upstream's pure half (P2-30): the socket URL (the only place the token is put in a URL), the channels a demand wants (per-ticker only, at most eight price and three gex), the ack and data parser, the backoff table, and the shapers that turn a socket payload into the same neutral frame and row the REST shapers give (`patchPxRow` and `mergePxRow` keep a focus name's option-flow columns current under a socket price).

| File | Exports | Suites |
|---|---|---|
| `shared/flows-rt-socket.js` | `shared/flows-rt-socket.js#SOCKET_CHANNELS`, `shared/flows-rt-socket.js#SOCKET_HOURS`, `shared/flows-rt-socket.js#SOCKET_LIMITS`, `shared/flows-rt-socket.js#SOCKET_TOPICS`, `shared/flows-rt-socket.js#backoffMs`, `shared/flows-rt-socket.js#channelTopic`, `shared/flows-rt-socket.js#gexBody`, `shared/flows-rt-socket.js#mergePxRow`, `shared/flows-rt-socket.js#newsBody`, `shared/flows-rt-socket.js#parseMessage`, `shared/flows-rt-socket.js#patchPxRow`, `shared/flows-rt-socket.js#shapePxTicks`, `shared/flows-rt-socket.js#shapeSocketFrame`, `shared/flows-rt-socket.js#socketPath`, `shared/flows-rt-socket.js#socketUrl`, `shared/flows-rt-socket.js#socketWindowOk`, `shared/flows-rt-socket.js#tickerOfPayload`, `shared/flows-rt-socket.js#wantChannels` | flows, flows-basis, flows-dossier-reads, flows-engine-budget, flows-ledger, flows-public and 6 more |

### `shared/flows-rt-hub.js`

`RtHub` (demand, roster, scheduler tick, merge, frames, degrade and resync, hello, status; no REST in it), `createRestUpstream` (the REST adapter: every path, cursor, rotation, deadline and 429 pause lives here), `createSocketUpstream` and `createCompositeUpstream` (the vendor socket upstream behind `FLOWS_RT_UPSTREAM`, shared by every member), `createHub`, `loadRosterFromD1` (one read-only batch) and the `Pulse` Durable Object class wiring hibernatable sockets and alarms to the hub. Imports no `cloudflare:workers`, so Node loads it.

| File | Exports | Suites |
|---|---|---|

### `shared/flows-rt-routes.js`

`serveRt`: `/api/rt/ws`, `/api/rt/snap`, `/api/rt/status` inside `route()`; the kill switches, the audience gate, the origin check, and the forward to the one named object.

| File | Exports | Suites |
|---|---|---|

### `assets/js/flows-rt.js`

The rail's browser half, attached as `FlowsUI.rt`: one WebSocket per tab with refcounted topics, the ladder socket, then `GET /api/rt/snap` every 5 s, then the pages' own heartbeats (and `off` when the rail answers 401, 403 or 404), per-topic sequence and epoch handling (a gap sends `{t:"rs"}` and holds the topic until a snapshot; a new epoch discards its state), `rt:<topic>` freshness entries that are dropped when the transport falls, rAF-conflated listeners (`FlowsUI.rt.on`), and the adapters the pages read: `strips()` (px rows mapped back to the `live:strips` body, `fields` from `meta.cols`), `quote(t)`, `market(base)`, `alerts()`, `news()`, `gex(t)`, `feeds(k)`. It never touches the DOM.

| File | Exports | Suites |
|---|---|---|
| `assets/js/flows-rt.js` |  | fixture-guard, flows-fixture-guard-render |

## flows-pipeline

The nightly pipeline, the live leg and their tools.

### `scripts/flows-legs/stamp.mjs`, `tests/flows-pipeline-golden.mjs`, `tests/fixtures-pipeline-golden.json`, `tests/lib/nightly-emit.mjs`

The nightly's pinned dry-run clock and the golden emit. `FLOWS_DRY_NOW` (an ISO instant ending in `Z`, honoured only with `--dry-run`, anything else throws) pins `stampNow()`/`stampDate()`, the one clock every payload stamp (`generatedAt`, `readAt`, `screenerReadAt`, `refreshedAt`) is read from; elapsed-time and deadline measures keep the wall clock, and the pipeline's own source scan fails on a bare `new Date().toISOString()`. `nightlyEmit()` builds the dry-run emit once (CI default clock `2026-08-24T21:45:00Z`) into `FLOWS_EMIT_DIR`, which `tests/run.mjs` sets to one directory per runner invocation and removes at the end, or into a temporary directory of its own; a mark holding a fingerprint of `scripts/` and `shared/` makes a stale directory rebuild. The eight emit consumers (`flows-card-render`, `flows-events-contract`, `flows-overlay-contract`, `flows-payload-shape`, `flows-positioning-contract`, `flows-strip`, `flows-ticker-contract`, `flows-unusual-contract`) read it instead of running their own. The golden suite (the first half of `test:pipeline`) hashes every emitted payload against the manifest, builds a second emit to prove two runs at one clock are byte-identical, and names the payload and top-level keys that moved; `node flows-pipeline-golden.mjs --write` regenerates the manifest after an intended change and `--against <emit dir of the old tree>` prints the leaf paths.

### `scripts/flows-pipeline.mjs`, `scripts/flows-nightly/*.mjs`, `scripts/flows-nightly/sections/*.mjs`

The nightly. The entry is the command line: the session prelude and the unranked-refresh early return, `runLiveMode`, `main` (one `ctx`, eleven awaited sections), and a re-export of every name the suites import, so no suite imports a module path. The modules sit in layers and an import may only point down: `flags` (the process flags and the `FLOWS_DRY_NOW` pin), `vendor-params` (what is asked of the vendor and the call budget), `vendor` (`uw`, the permit queue, the read pace and the pool), `store` (ingest URL, the live credential, read, publish, retire, prune and the dated-key grammar), `clock`, `rank` (scoring and shaping, `enrich`), `fixtures` (the dry run's synthetic vendor), `archive` (board memory, the dated walk, the record's inputs, the roster ledger), then the sections, `runUniverse` to `runClose` (`runCompute` between `runFocus` and `runClose`), none importing another; `stages` is a leaf. A section destructures what it reads from `ctx` and returns what a later one needs with `Object.assign(ctx, ...)`; `tests/flows-legs-contract.mjs` holds each declaration to its module, the layer rule and the no-comment rule for the whole tree. A test reads the nightly through `nightlySource()` (entry, then every module, each marked), `nightlySlice()` (throws when a slice crosses a module) or `nightlyExecution()` (the entry with each section spliced in where it runs) from `tests/lib/source-scan.mjs`, never by reading one file.

### `scripts/flows-nightly/stages.mjs`

The nightly's stage table (`STAGES`: id, fatal or isolated, `needs`, declared `publishes`) and `createStageRunner`: `step(id)` for a fatal stage, `run(id, work, onError)` for an isolated one (the failure is recorded and handed to `onError`, a fatal one rethrows), `skip`, `note(key)` from `publish`, `detail(fields)` (at most six extra numbers or short strings on the open stage's record, never a record field), `finish`, `records()`; given a `cpu` clock the runner adds `cpu` (whole milliseconds of the process's CPU time) to every record, and the dry run pins that clock to 0 with the stamp. `meta` is the last write of the nightly, after the health gate, and carries `stages` and `health` (`healthRecord`); `publishedStore.meta` is set earlier so the brief still reads it. The pipeline's pipeline-contract block holds the table to the source (every id once, in order, fatal stages stepped and isolated ones run) and to the dry run (every key declared, `meta` last).

### `scripts/flows-nightly/sections/compute.mjs`

The compute stage, an isolated stage after `card-x` and before `roster`, once the vendor reads are done: it imports no vendor client, store or archive, so it cannot call the vendor or publish. `computeNames` runs the registered `COMPUTE_JOBS` (frozen, empty until a model brings one: `{ id, run(ticker, ctx) }`) over the carded names one at a time with a turn of the event loop between names; a name that spends more than `COMPUTE_NAME_CPU_MS` (1,000) of CPU is named in `over`, the stage stops before the next name once its share (names times that budget) is spent or the run's deadline has passed, and a job that throws costs that name that job only. Values are kept by job and ticker on `ctx.computed` for a later stage to publish. The stage record carries `cpu`, `names`, `jobs`, `over`, `failed` and `stopped`.

### `scripts/flows-ws-probe.mjs`, `.github/workflows/flows-ws-probe.yml`, `tests/flows-ws-probe-contract.mjs`

The vendor socket probe, dispatch only: handshake three ways, one connection per channel from one list led by a negative control (`w03_nonexistent_channel_zz`), five firehoses at 5 s each, concurrent connections, joins per connection, lag against each frame's own stamp; per channel messages and bytes a second, inter-arrival p50/p95 and `created_at - executed_at` for prints. It prints key names and statistics, never payload values or the token, and its start record carries the computed duration the workflow's 30-minute timeout must cover. The contract runs it against a fake vendor that speaks the WebSocket protocol by hand.

### `scripts/flows-legs/calibration.mjs`, `tests/flows-calibration.mjs`

The track record of the engine's ideas. The pipeline archives each night's lead idea as a trial (`ideaRecord`: legs, fill, spot, both chances of profit) inside `ideas:<date>`; `runCalibration` reads the earlier dates, settles each identity (ticker, family, legs, expiry) once from the daily close on its expiry with the engine's own payoff and profit intervals, folds the outcome into cluster (ISO expiry week) and reliability accumulators, writes `ideas-out:<date>` then `calib`, and fetches at most 15 closes a night for names that left the deep set. No look-ahead: only dates before the run, bars cut at the run, an expiry on a holiday settles on the last close before it. Calendars and ideas across a split are excluded by name; ideas with no close are counted, never dropped. `calib` shows reliability only from effective n 100. `flows-stats.js` is the P1-51a leaf, byte-identical to the one on its own branch.

## flows-client

Flows pages, scripts and styles.

### `assets/js/flows-fresh.js`

The client freshness helper (`FlowsUI.freshFrom`, `heartbeat`); every key a heartbeat reads registers its server verdict with the pill, which is the worst case over its sources, so a page needs no line per region. The pill says Live only when the market is open, the page has at least one entry registered `role: "primary"` (the rail registers the topics a page connects for: `px` where it asks for it) and every primary entry is inside its own live window with none stale (`FlowsUI.freshAll`, in `flows-ui.js`); the pill repaints itself when that window closes, a page with no primary entry never says Live and reads `Fresh · <age of the newest price read>`, and a `secondary` entry that is not live is listed in the details and demotes the pill to Fresh only when it is stale.

### `assets/js/flows-link.js`, `tests/flows-link.mjs`

`FlowsUI.link`, the one selection and time-cursor mechanism, added to the existing global. `flows-ui.js` carries a queue stub; the first `join` (or any `emit` or `cursor`) loads this body from beside `flows-ui.js` with the same `?v=` and `attach` drains the queue. `join(owner, {channel A, B, C or null, kinds, clocks, on})` returns `{leave}`; `emit(owner, {kind ticker, sector, strike, expiry, side or time, value, source})` is refused unless the value passes the kind's validator; the last emission per channel wins and each member is called at most once per animation frame, never for its own emission; a member that joins late is handed the current selection; focus never moves; announcements go through one polite live region at most every five seconds; `cursor({tau, clock known or event})` carries its clock and a member that lists only the other clock is not handed it and the page is told; `describe()` is `{scene, mode, tau, clock, selection}` in at most 256 bytes. The state is the URL hash `#l=A:t=NVDA;B:s=XLK;tc=known@2026-10-05T14:41:00Z` and nothing else: no browser storage. A second window shares it only when asked: `popout(path)` (a path on this origin) adds a random `w=<16 hex>` group token to the hash, opens the path on the current selection and joins a `BroadcastChannel` named for the token; the other window opens on that hash and joins the same channel, each local change travels once as a delta, a message that is not a valid selection is ignored, and a received change is never sent back. The per-view pop-out routes (the Network, the Now list) are not built. The test is Chromium with `page.route` and no server.

### `tests/flows-weight.mjs`, `tests/flows-defer-render.mjs`

`FlowsUI.defer(group, {when, el, label})` loads a group of scripts after the page's `load` event and returns a promise that resolves when they have run. A group is declared in the page as one `<i hidden data-defer="<group>" data-src="${v(path)}">` element per file (a list in one `data-src` is refused: the version scan cannot see past its first URL) and runs in declared order, one file after another: the next is requested only when the one before it has run, and the first failure stops the group, so a file never runs ahead of one it follows and a retry resumes at the file that failed. `when: "idle"` (default) waits for `requestIdleCallback` with a 1.5 s timeout; `when: "visible"` waits for `el` to come within 200 px of the viewport, then idle. Under `navigator.connection.saveData` the slot (`el`) gets a status line and a button and nothing is fetched until it is pressed; a failed fetch removes its script element, requests nothing further from the group and puts an unavailable line with Retry above the slot's own content, and the promise stays pending until a retry works. A second call for a group returns the first promise. `flows-ui.js` has one script loader, `loadJs`, shared by `defer` and the `FlowsUI.link` stub; `flows-dock.js` has the only other. The Unusual page loads `flows-net.js` this way (`defer("net", {when: "visible", el: #uaNet, label})`): the host is `.fn-host.is-slot` in the served HTML, so its height is reserved at first paint by container-query bands in `flows-net.css` (the mounted network's height at each card width, measured; the header's two-row wrap under 540 px too). When the alerts arrive empty, withheld, pending or failed, `flows-unusual.js` swaps `is-slot` for `.is-quiet`, whose own bands are the height of the network's empty state (about 450 to 790 px against 1,000 to 1,360 for the full scene), so the card is not a one-line message in a 1,100 px box once the network has mounted and nothing below it moves when it does; a feed with rows swaps back. Until the module mounts it holds a table of the eight largest names by flagged premium with their lean by the convention, written by `flows-unusual.js` from the alerts it already has. `flows-defer-render` holds that nothing below the card moves when the network arrives, that the reservation never falls short of the mounted height by more than 6 px nor leaves more than 50 px empty at eleven widths on a fresh load, in the full scene and in the empty one (an empty feed and a 500 at 1440 and 390 px move nothing below the card, with a layout shift under 0.1), that a two-file group whose first file fails once requests nothing of the second and runs both in order after Retry, that Data Saver and a 404 leave the table in place, and that the request starts after the load event. `tests/flows-weight.mjs` weighs a route as its eager scripts plus the files `DEFERRED` lists for it (declared groups), the dock's `flows-ask.js` and the `flows-link.js` body, against a stated per-route allowance; it scans every `flows-*.js` for a second loader and for a `.defer("group")` call the route's `DEFERRED` entry does not list, runs the declaration checker on eight negative fixtures (a space and a comma in `data-src`, a file missing from `DEFERRED`, a table entry with no element on the page, an unversioned `data-src`, an element that is not hidden, a file that is also a script src, a file other than the table's), and prints the headroom of each route against the 1 KiB floor with the pending `EAGER_KERNEL` entries subtracted on live routes: the routes under the floor are the `BELOW_FLOOR` list, each with the row that relieves it, and the list must equal the computed set in both directions. The ticker's ceiling carries a named `TICKER_RAISE` (OD-08, 1,613 B, the cost of `FlowsUI.defer` on a route that defers no group): it is held at that size, asserted to be deleted as soon as the ticker defers as many bytes of group files as it was raised by, and printed on every run; the kernel entries state their size and a landed entry is measured against it (`FlowsUI.defer` 1.6 KB, so the V and L share of the kernel is held at 3.8 KB under the named `KERNEL_LIMIT` decision, not the 3.1 KB of section 2.10). `flows-defer-render` is Chromium with `page.route` and no server.

### `assets/js/flows-ticker-surface.js`, `tests/flows-surface-render.mjs`, `tests/lib/surface-card.mjs`

The ticker's Surface & terrain view, the second tab of the chart panel (`FlowsUI.tickerSurface`: `mount` and the pure `model.surface` and `model.terrain`), declared as `<i hidden data-defer="surface" id="ftSurface">` when `FLOWS_SCENES` is `on` (or `owner` and the member is an owner) and loaded by `FlowsUI.defer` when the tab opens. It replaces the old scenes module and the dock's Volatility surface and Gamma terrain buttons. Rows are the union of the gamma grid's expiries and the engine's, joined by `FlowsUI.tickerGlance.model.expiries`, so the chip row, the strike rail on the price view and these scenes read one list; a chip carries the engine's fit word (FITTED SVI, SVI, REPAIRED or NO FIT). Terrain: dealer gamma by strike and expiry as columns, heights to the 98th percentile (clipped at 1.25 times it, marked), a cell the vendor did not return a dashed outline and never a zero. Surface: the engine's stored SVI read through `FlowsQuant.sliceTotalVariance` in k = ln(K/F), total variance interpolated linearly in time at a fixed strike between consecutive fitted expiries (`between`), rows that span an expiry with no fit faded, an expiry with no fit a ring at its at-the-money level and never a curve, and a calendar check that scans total variance against time. Both share one camera (drag or arrow keys, Home or Reset view), strike, expiry and level labels go through one declutter pass that drops what would overlap and records every box, canvases are described with a table fallback, and on a phone the three columns are swipeable cards. A card with neither a smile nor a gamma grid shows the withheld reason. `tests/flows-surface-render.mjs` (Chromium, `page.route`, no server) holds the strike rail to the price axis within 1 px, the expiry cursor and its one-sigma band, 25-delta strikes, risk reversal and butterfly to an independent solve and to the engine's stored figures, null cells, thinned time-axis labels, the tab and keyboard, the calendar check, withheld states and thirteen planted wrong builds. `tests/lib/surface-card.mjs` builds its synthetic card.

### `assets/js/flows-ticker-glance.js`, `tests/flows-glance-render.mjs`

The ticker glance's hero chart and six instrument cards, attached as `FlowsUI.tickerGlance` (`mount(card, hosts, opts)` and the pure `model(card, range, extra)` with `layout`, `cluster`, `cards`, `readout`, `describe`, `claims` and `evidence` beside it). `mount` fills two hosts, `ftGlanceChart` and `ftGlanceCards`, and returns `{ setRange, update, destroy }`; `opts` is `{ reading, summary, range }` plus `cardX` and `tape`, the two payloads the card alone does not carry (the dossier companion for earnings, short volume, insiders, the cone and NOPE, and the live tape for the session's cumulative premium), and `update({ reading, claims, cardX, tape, card })` repaints. The chart is one SVG sized from its host: closes for 20, 63, 126 or 252 sessions back, today, and 20 sessions forward in which Q (the card's implied range, orange, solid edges) and P (its realised range, blue, dashed) widen with the square root of time from the variance per session each range implies, both stepped at the report (half a session after the close for an after-close report, before for a pre-open one) by the implied event move for Q and the median realised report move for P, and no step when the report is not inside the horizon; the call wall, put wall, max pain, flip and spot as lines with right-edge labels placed by one clustering rule (labels merge into a stack a label height apart, centred on the mean of their levels, held inside the plot); the gamma dealers added today by strike as bars scaled to the largest, blue long and hatched orange short, under a header with the book figure on the vendor's convention; the Flows score by session under the price on one scale, today's bar emphasised; and a legend row that names, instead of drawing, every layer the card cannot supply. The chart is one tab stop whose arrow keys, Home, End and Escape move a crosshair across the sessions with the reading in a polite live region; its accessible description carries the close, both moves, the report, the levels and the dealer clause. The six cards (Signal, Volatility, Flow, Positioning, Earnings, Range) take each figure from the same card fields the lower modules read, show an em dash with the reason in the footer line when the input is absent, and become 68 px tiles of a title, a figure and a unit below 641 px of container width. Linking is a document event, `ft:focus` with `{ id, source }` (`mark`, `card` or `claim`): with a reading, claims are numbered in reading order (the `now` section, the drivers, the tensions, then the watch list, at most four, or `opts.claims`), `EVIDENCE` maps fact ids to layers and cards by prefix, marks stand on the first layer a claim evidences (flip, report, gamma, walls, cone), every layer and card declares `data-claims`, and a focused claim keeps its evidence and steps everything else back; with no reading there are no marks, no `data-claims` and no dimming. Every colour is a class on a token under the `.ftg-` rules of `flows-ticker.css`. `tests/flows-glance-render.mjs` (Chromium, `page.route`, no server) holds the cone, the step, the labels, the bars and every card figure to independent computations on the dry-run emit's real card, the absent-input states, the range control, the dimming, keyboard, sizing at 1440, 1920, 390 and 320 px, reduced motion, and thirteen planted wrong builds. Strike rail (current): when the card carries expiries, the right of the chart is no longer the aggregate gamma profile but a rail on the same price axis (`model.expiries`, `defaultExpiry`, `skew`, `miniRail`): dealer gamma by strike for the chosen expiry (a cell not returned is a dashed outline, never a zero), the smile with 25-delta put and call markers and the quotes the card holds, a one-sigma band of spot times exp(plus or minus the expiry's at-the-money volatility times the square root of T), and clickable expiry ticks on the forward axis (a radio group; labels thinned so none overlap the today label) that move the expiry cursor and announce `ft:expiry`. Under about 600 px the rail drops below as an expiry chip row and two mini charts. The 25-delta strikes come from `FlowsQuant.skewMetrics`, exported by the read bundle with `sliceTotalVariance`.

### `assets/js/flows-ticker-movelab.js`, `tests/flows-movelab-render.mjs`

The Move lab on the ticker page, for every member. `tickerPage` always declares `<i hidden data-defer="movelab" id="ftMoveLab">`; `flows-ticker.js` adds `buildMoveLab`, which makes the `m-movelab` module for a card that is not an index or a fund and asks for the group with `FlowsUI.defer("movelab", {when: "visible"})`, handing the file the card, the card-x record, the card's spot and its daily closes. The file adds `FlowsUI.moveLab` (`mount`, and the pure `model`, `inputOf`, `rowOf`, `returnsOf`, `counted`, `frequency` and `fmt` the suite reads) and reads `GET /api/flows/universe?cols=ed,f2,f3,sig,t` once, only when the module nears the viewport. Three blocks. A size ladder of 1, 2, 3 and 4 times the name's typical daily move (`sig`, a fraction the nightly run publishes at scale 1e5; a figure of a quarter of the price or more is refused as a unit slip), each size with the price after a rise and after a fall (`spot x (1 +/- k x sig)`, to the cent), how many of the last 252 sessions moved that far (`f2` and `f3` for two and three times, report days left out; the card's own closes for one and four times, report days kept in, scaled to 252 when fewer returns are held and refused under 200) and how rare that is, in the sentence `frequencyText` writes, floor of 1 in 250 and an interval from five hits included, never a model probability. The options market's own implied move to the nearest end-of-week expiry from `panels.pricedMove` (`movePerc`, `low`, `high`), printed as the market's number and not a forecast. The earnings report, tagged event-contaminated when it is 21 sessions or fewer away (the card's `earnings.next.sessions`, else the universe's `ed`), with the market's report move. An absent input is a dash and its reason (no column, no row, too little history, no quote), never a zero, and a failed universe read offers Retry. A closing line says what the lab is not. No vendor call, no model call, no new global, no statistics jargon in its copy. `tests/flows-movelab-render.mjs` (Chromium, `page.route`, no server) holds every printed figure to an exact integer computation, `frequencyText` for all 253 counts and `sigmaRef`'s returns for the card counts, then the absent states, keyboard, touch hit areas, contrast, reduced motion and the 320 to 1440 px layout, and fails the planted wrong builds (the typical day taken as a percent, up and down swapped, the floor missing, and fifteen more).

### `assets/js/flows-net.js`, `assets/css/flows-net.css`

The flow network, attached as `FlowsUI.net` (`model`, `mount`, `of`) and loaded only by the unusual page, whose hero it is. `model(rows, o)` is pure: each flagged window's premium is split by the vendor's ask-side and bid-side attribution (the rest, left between the quotes or never split, is Unattributed) and routed side, sector (the nightly universe's; the top five and Other once there are more than six), name (the largest ten, fewer on a narrow screen, and Other names), then lean (calls at ask and puts at bid bullish, calls at bid and puts at ask bearish, by convention) or expiry horizon (calendar days from the window's own Eastern date), so every layer, every hop and every node's outgoing edges sum to the same premium. Every layer is ranked by premium, largest at the top (rank numerals on the names), with the residual buckets last after a gap 1.6 times the normal one whatever their size (Other or Other names, then No sector, Sector unread, Sector pending; No expiry in the Expiry output). `mount(host)` draws v3 on Canvas 2D, laid out to `programme/NETWORK-DIRECTION.md`: money is the shape, a sphere's area is its premium (r = 6 + 28 sqrt(v / the largest node) px at 1440, never under 9) and a band's width is its premium (0.75 px per $1M, floor 1.25 px; the scale tightens as 56 px over the largest node so its bands never outgrow its sphere above a $75M node), one scale for every node and band and a legend row under the scene that draws three reference circles and three band swatches on that same scale; bands are filled Sankey bands stacked at each node in rank order so a node's outgoing widths add to its total, screen-blended in the lean colours (#2ee37a, #ff5a4e, #4ea2ff; Other and No sector in slate #6f7d99); spheres are lit sprites with a specular highlight and a rim, a halo that grows with premium, fog by depth and a soft shadow on a perspective floor grid; four glass planes stand behind the layers on a shallow arc. The scene is laid out in rest screen pixels (four columns at equal spacing centred on the midline, every stack centred with equal gaps, chips 22 px tall on one x edge per column, all four headers carrying the same total, controls above and the legend below, nothing else inside the scene) and lifted into world space with the inverse of the rest camera, so the symmetry holds to the pixel at rest and the Ambient sway (8 degrees over 40 s, pitch fixed at 11, reduced at layout to the widest amplitude at which no chip meets a sphere, a chip or a header, sampled at sixteen phases with the spheres at their 1.03 breathing maximum) supplies the depth. Below 640 px the layers become rows (ranks left to right, chips under their spheres in as many tiers as keep them apart) on a 0.62 scale; between 640 and 1100 px the scale is 0.85 or 0.72 and the chips two lines when one line would not clear the next column. Motion has two classes, and the loop runs only while something moves. Live (every viewer): a window that arrives while the page is open flares its name (a 1.2 s ring) and lights its path, a band carries pulses only for premium that arrived in the last five minutes (one to four by that band's share of the busiest band's arrivals, zero when nothing arrived), ranks glide when new data reorders them, and the camera moves only while the reader drags it plus inertia that is over inside 8 s; at rest the paint census is zero. Ambient (the Ambient button, the `ambient` tag and a legend key reading `ambient motion, not data`): pulses along every band at 7 s down to 2.4 s a lap and one to four by premium, spheres breathing 1.00 to 1.03 in rank-staggered phases, and the yaw sway; it opens on for the owner's account (`data-ambient="1"` from the page, so Live and Ambient run together), on outside the session (the freshness pill reads closed) so the scene is never a still image, and otherwise off, the reader's own click remembered in a first-party preference cookie `fn_ambient` (Path `/flows`, one year, the pattern of `flows_gate`; `localStorage` is reserved for `IEWTStorage`) and winning over both; `ambient:on` and `ambient:off` bubble from the host. No pass uses the `lighter` composite operation, and pulses are computed from the loop's clock with no per-frame allocation (batched by width into at most twenty strokes). hover, focus or a table row lights a subgraph and dims the rest, with a readout of the node's figure and share at the top right; Freeze stops the pulses, breathing, flares and sway in both classes (its label swaps to Resume and it carries no `aria-pressed`, so a screen reader hears one state; it is hidden under reduced motion, which draws one still frame). Below 640 px the four layer headers are drawn as dark chips with no band crossing them, and a hover readout always clears the headers. When the web font arrives after the first draw (`document.fonts.ready` and `loadingdone`, debounced to a frame and acted on only when the measured width of a reference string changed) the labels are measured again and the layout rebuilt, with keyboard focus kept. Under 540 px of module width the legend is two groups, each a title row over one row of three items, and the paths table rebalances its columns so no header truncates at 320 px. A pointer or horizontal touch drag orbits the camera (yaw within 55 degrees, 30 on a phone, pitch within 22, with inertia) but never past the angle at which a chip would meet a sphere, a chip or a header (`fits`, 6 bisections), so at 1440 it stops near 27 to 36 degrees; a double click or tap, R or 0 on a node, or Recentre returns it. The painter's order is floor, shadows, planes, bands, pulses, headers, spheres back to front, labels, and it stops when the tab is hidden or the canvas is off screen. `destroy()` cancels the loop, aborts the universe fetch, disconnects its observers and removes its listeners. The node hit targets (one roving tab stop after Freeze and Recentre, arrow keys between nodes, names linking to the ticker page), the canvas's spoken summary and the table of the 24 largest paths are its accessible half. It reads `/api/flows/universe` once for sectors and nothing else; the unusual controller hands it the alerts it already holds.

### `assets/js/flows-export.js`, `tests/flows-export.mjs`

The export menu, attached to `FlowsUI` the way `flows-fresh.js` does (a re-frozen copy), loaded only on the boards (`/flows/long/`, `/flows/short/`, `/flows/watch/`) and the desk, before their controllers. `FlowsUI.csv` writes RFC 4180 with CRLF, a byte order mark, the unit in each header and two padded footer rows (source and as-of, then the export instant); a string cell that starts with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab or CR gets a leading apostrophe, a finite number is written bare, and null, undefined, NaN and Infinity are empty. The menu offers CSV for the view as the reader sees it (sort and filter applied) and SVG and PNG of the board map and the desk frontier, serialised with computed colours so no custom property is left unresolved. Exports are limited to the site's own scores, model outputs and aggregates (OD-30): the board's last price and day change, and the desk's quoted premium, are not columns. The suite is Chromium with `page.route` stubs, no server, and reads each download back through an independent CSV parser.
